FACTS:

(1) Grounds and other factors set-out in the memorandum of appeal clearly establish that the respondent No.1 had no right to file the writ petition questioning the order of respondent No.1 in respect of order dated: 02.11.2020, passed in
C. Dis. RAP./SR/33/2019, (Annexure-A) to the writ petition. Further it is submitted that-

a) the property described in the power of attorney given by Smt. Padma Hegde to her agent was in respect of land bearing Sy. No.148/7 measuring 2 acres 78 cents, situated in Yadathadi grama,

™" Brahmavara Taluk, Udupi District, but in the plaint in O. S. No.27/2012 and the decree passed by the Civil Court referred to suit property has Sy. No.148/P1 measuring 1 acre 88 cents of Yadathadi grama, Brahmavara Taluk, Udupi District. In the premises, the identity of the property was not established,

b) Agreement of sale dated: 15-06-2009 was an inadmissible document, in as much as, the document was not stamped as required under Section 5-G of the Karnataka Stamp Act.
Particularly, as the possession of the property was

- delivered under the agreement to the respondent No.1,

10- 4 c) said agreement of sale dated: 15.06.2009 was not registered under the provisions of the Registration Act, more so, when the possession of the property was given to the respondent No.1.
Hon'ble Trial Court with passed the decree in O. S.
No.27/2012, did not take into consideration of the above aspect and therefore such a decree does not confer any right in favour respondent No.1 and therefore, respondent No.1 has not right to question, the sale deed in favour of the appellant in respect of property bearing Sy. No.148/28 Fe measuring 1 acre 88 cents of Yadathadi grama, Brahmavara Taluk, Udupi District, and also question the orders of the revenue authorities making Khatha of the said property in the name of the appellant.
WHEREFORE, the Appellant has approached Division Bench for redress.

IV. FACTS:

(i) Respondent No.1, on the basis of alleged Lal unregistered agreement of sale dated: 15-06- 2009, purported to have been executed by one Kishan Hegde, on behalf of Smt. Padma Hegde, agreed to purchase the land in Sy. No.148/P1 for the consideration stated therein. Such Agreement of Sale, whereby possession of the property to be sold was given to the said respondent No.1, is not registered as required under the provisions of the Registration Act. On the date of Sale Agreement as well as, decree passed in O. S. No.27/2012, RTC extract was not standing in the name of Padma Hegde in respect of suit property. Copy of the Agreement of sale

7 dated: 15-06-2009 is produced herewith.

-14-

(ii) It is stated that GPA dated:16-05-2009 executed by the said Smt. Padma Hegde in favour of Kishan Hegde states that the attorney was empowered to sell her property bearing Sy. No.148/7 measuring 2 acres 39%2 cents, situated in Yadathadi grama, Brahmavara Taluk, Udupi District. Copy of GPA dated:

16.05.2009 is herewith produced.

(ii) Respondent No.1, instituted O. S. No.27/2012 in the court of Senior Civil Judge at Kundapura, for specific performance of the Agreement of sale dated: 15.06.2009 by impleading Smt. Padma

» Hegde and her attorney Kishan Hegde are parties.

(iv) Padma Hegde, defendant No.1 in the said suit, contested the case by filing written statement whereby, she denied and disputed the claim of the respondent No.1.

(v) Ld. Senior Civil Judge at Kundapura disposed of
O. S. No.27/2012, by a judgment and decree dated: 15.07.2015, whereby, the said suit of the respondent No.1 was decreed with a direction to defendant No.1 Padma Hegde to execute the sale deed in respect of suit property bearing

Sy. no.148/P1, measuring 1 acre 88 cents of Yadathadi grama, Brahmavara Taluk, Udupi

-15- District, failing which respondent No.1 is entitled - to get the sale deed executed through the process of court.

(vi) It is submitted that, the said Ld. Court, ignoring the said agreement of sale was grossly under- stamped and not registered under the provisions of the Registration Act, particularly when the possession of the property to be sold was given to the respondent No.1, passed the decree in
O. S. No. 27/2012. It is stated that the said judgment and decree of the said Ld. Court is non-est in law and the same does not confer any

¥ right in favour of respondent No.1 in respect of the suit property.

(vii) The said Padma Hegde, defendant No.1 in
0. S. No0.27/2012, questioned the judgment and decree of the court of Civil Judge, Kundapura, dated: 15-07-2015, by filing Regular Appeal in the court of the Addl. District and Sessions Judge, Udupi, and such appeal was registered as
R. A.3/2015. In such First Appeal there was stay of judgement and decree of the Trial Court.

(vill) Respondent No.1 had filed criminal case against the said Smt. Padma Hegde, in the court of II

-16- offences punishable under Sections 177, 420 = R/w 34 of IPC. Smt. Padma Hegde, filed Section 482 of petition in the High Court of Karnataka at Bengaluru, being Crl. Petn. No.8926/2017, for quashing the order dated:24.12.2016, passed in
C. C. No.1235/2016, by the said court of II Addl.
Judge & Judicial Magistrate 1° Class Kundapura.
In the High Court the said Padma Hegde and Respondent No.1 settled the matter inter-alia, on the ground that the dispute between the parties is settled. Consequent, the High Court vide order dated: 02.07.2017, quashed the criminal proceedings filed against the = Smt. Padma Hegde on the - ground of arrangement between the said parties. These facts clearly establish collusion between the Smt. Padma Hegde and respondent No.1 and such collusive action at the effect of jeoparadising the interest of the appellant.
(ix) It is submitted that, the appellant herein, under a registered sale deed dated: 08.06.2015, executed by the said Padma Hegde through her GPA holder Nikhil Hegde, for valuable consideration of Rs.11,28,000/-, purchased property bearing Sy. No.48/28 measuring 1 - Acres 88 cents, situated in Yadathadi grama, Brahmavara Taluk, Udupi District.

V. Appellant herein declares that, he has not impugned ‘ the order dated: 08.04.2024 of the Hon’ble Single Judge of this Hon’ble Court, before any other bench of this Hon'ble Court or any other authority, excepting this writ appeal.

VI. The Appellant herein impugns the aforesaid order of the Ld. Single Judge dated: 08.04.2024 of the Single Judge of this Hon‘ble Court passed in W. P. No.
14741/2020 (KLR-RES), inter-alia, on the grounds urged herein and also the grounds that may be

advanced at the time of arguments: -

GROUNDS

¥ (i) There is error apparent on the face of the record in the impugned order of the Ld. Single Judge, in as much as, the Ld. Single Judge has not taken into account the various grounds urged in the synopsis and also the statement of objections and therefore, the impugned order suffers from factual and legal infirmity. In the premises, this is a fit case for interfering with

the order of the Ld. Single Judge.

(ii) It is obvious from the face of the records, being the description of the property set-out in GPA dated: 15.06.2009, agreement of sale dated:
15.06.2009 and decree passed by the Civil

Court in O. S. No.27/2012, vis-a-vis, the property purchased by the appellant vide registered sale

-22- deed dated: 08-06-2015. Respondent No.1 has failed to establish the identity of the property.
In such circumstances, quashing of the order dated: 02-11-2020 is without jurisdiction.

(iii) Ld. Single Judge, failed to note, that the agreement of sale dated: 15-06-2009, is grossly under-stamped and secondly such agreement of sale was not registered in terms of Section 17(1) and (1)(a) of the Registration Act, particularly, when the respondent No.1 got possession of the property to the sold under such agreement. Consequently, the civil court

2 with passed the decree dated:15.07.2015 in
O. S. No. 27/2012, failed to look into these legal aspects and thereby, such decree cannot be basis for the respondent No.1 to question the sale deed dated: 08.06.2015 in favour of the appellant.

(iv) The judgment and decree dated: 15.07.2015 passed in O. S. No.27/2012 by the Civil Judge at Kundapura, suffered from legal infirmity and such decree is void ab-initio. In the circumstances, the appellant in question the legality of the said decree in any proceedings, including collateral proceedings.

-23-
(v) Observation of the Ld. Single Judge in the impugned order that respondent No.2 could not have reviewed the earlier order dated:
11.12.2017, by allowing Revision Petition No.
C. Dis. RAP/SR.33/2019 dated: 02.11.2020, since the Karnataka Land Revenue Act do not permit review of the order dated: 11.12.2017, is not proper. It is submitted that having regard to Section 24 & 25 of R/w Section 136(3) of Karnataka Land Revenue Act, the Revenue Court has inherent power to correct its order. In the circumstances, the order of Ld. Single Judge accepting writ petition and quashing impugned ' order dated: 02-11-2020, deserves to be set- aside.

(vi) The Ld. Single Judge failed to appreciate the collusive arrangement between Smt. Padma Hegde and Respondent No.1 in the matter of settling the Criminal action and by virtue of the order of the High Court, there is every possibility that Smt. Padma Hegde would withdraw R. A. No.3/2015 pending in the court of Addl. District and Sessions Judge, Udupi. This collusive action on the part of the said parties

will jeoparadise the interest of the appellant.
(vii) The Final Order of the Ld. Single Judge directing the revenue authorities to maintain revenue

-24- records in the name of original owner Smt.
Padma Hegde, would result in irreparable hardship and injury to the appellant.

(viii) That in the above fact the impugned order requires to be corrected in the exercises of appellate jurisdiction for rendering justice to the appellant.

(ix) Finding of the Ld. Single Judge, that court or quasi judicial authority having jurisdiction to adjudicate on merit can review only if the court or quasi judicial authority in vested with power of review, by express provision or necessary

v implication, humbly stated, may not be proper position of law, in as much as, the revenue courts have implicit power to review its order to advance justice, and more so, when the order of respondent No.2. dated: 11.12.2017, passed in
C. Dis. RAP/ 36-2016-17, records that, the sale deed in favour of Respondent No.5, despite stay order of the civil court dated: 19.01.2013, passed in O0. S. No.27/2012, is bad in law. Such, observation of the respondent No.2 is without jurisdiction and therefore, such order has been reviewed by respondent No.2 subsequently.

- (x) The impugned order of the Ld. Single Judge is against law, facts and probabilities of the case

i -25- and therefore, the same is liable to corrected in this appeal.
(xi) Impugned order, in the facts and circumstances, deserves to be interfered to advance justice to the appellant.
VII. Copy application in respect of impugned order dated:
08-04-2024 passed in W. P. No.14741/2020 (KLR- RES), was applied on 10.04.2024, 09.04.2024 was holiday and the applicant was notified regarding delivery of the certified copy 23.04.2024 and it was delivered on 24.04.2024. Hence, the appeal filed v today is in time.
PRAYER
In the aforesaid facts and circumstances, it is humbly prayed on behalf of the appellant that this Hon‘ble Court be pleased to -
a) Call for the records in W. P. No.14741/2020 (KLR- RES), including the impugned order dated:
08.04.2024 passed by the Ld. Sing Judge of the Hon'ble High Court in the said writ petition, for verifying the factual correctness and legality of the same;
- b) Set-aside the said impugned order dated:08-04- 2024, passed in W. P. No.14741/2020 (KLR-RES) by

-26- allowing this appeal and dismiss the said writ hd petition with costs throughout;
c) Grant such other relief/s as this Hon‘ble Court may deem fit and proper, including keeping intact all contentions of the appellant before any other Forum to advance justice to the appellant.

ORDER
R, DEVDAS J., (ORAL):
The petitioner is aggrieved of the impugned order dated 02.11.2020 at Annexure-A, which is an order in review passed by the respondent-Deputy Commissioner, Udupi District.
2. A brief background may be required to understand susie the dispute that was brought before the Deputy ey. Sommissioner. One Smt. Padma Hegde acquired 2.78 - f a) sas £0* \2.
a9, 's he olay
2 5 K
2 HOTA
ee oe

Acres of land in terms of a Partition Deed dated 05.01.2008. Smt. Padma Hegde, disposed of various pieces of land under six sale deeds during the year 2009 to 2020.
What remained with Smt. Padma Hegde was 1.9450 Acres.
The petitioner herein had entered into an agreement dated 15.06.2009 in respect of 1.88 Acres through the Power of Attorney Holder of Smt. Padma Hedge. When the owner of 4 the property did not come forward for execuling the absolute sale deed, the petitioner herein filed a suit in
O. S. No.27/2012 for Specific Perfurmance of the Contract.
3. It is contended that an order of Temporary Injunction was granted by the Senior Civil Judge at Kundapura, restraining the landlord from alienating the suit schedule property during the pendency of the suit.
However, it is alleged that during the pendency of the suit and the order of Temporary Injunction being in force, the 4"" respondent herein who was the Advocate appearing for GRE OPER Paame Hedge purchased the suit schedule property 27 PEERO another Power of Attorney Holder of Smt. Padma

JID
OF sco NC: 2024: KHC:14503 " Bea ert WP No. 14741 of 2020 Hegde, who was also her brother. The sale deed was got executed on 08.06.2015. Nevertheless, the suit was decreed on 15.07.2015 with a direction to the defendant- Smt. Padma Hegde to execute a sale deed in favour of the plaintiff/petitioner. Smt. Padma Hegde filed a regular appeal in R. A. No.3/2015 before the Additional District and Sessions Judge, calling in question the order passed by the trial court. In the meanwhile, the 4°" respondent herein sought to get his name entered in the land records. The ¥ respondent-Tahsildar allowed the application and issued a direction to enter the name of the 4" respondent in the land record on the strength of the registered sale deed.
The said order of the Tahsildar was questioned by the petitioner herein before the Assistant Commissioner in RRT(2) SR 31/2016-17, wherein the 4" respondent was arraigned as 2" respondent. The Assistant Commissioner dismissed the appeal and consequently, the petitioner approached the Deputy Commissioner inveking the pA _# reisiona powers under Section 136(3) of the Karnataka [232/.., ie een Revenue Act, 1964 (hereinafter referred to as ‘the Y Ho”

3) “5 Ob emo NC; 2024: KHC:14503 sees WP No. 14741 of 2020 Act, 1964", for short). The Deputy Commissioner allowed the Revision Petition by order dated 11.12.2017 while setting aside the order of the Special Tahsildar and directed restoration of the name of Smt. Padma Hegde in the land records.
4. Learned Counsel for the petitioner submits that although an order was passed by the Deputy —_ v Commissioner on 11.12.2017 directing restoration of name of Smt. Padma Hegde in the land records, in the meanwhile, the 4 respondent herein filed applications before the Additional DistricL and Sessions Court in the regular appeal seeking to implead himself. Learned Counsel submits that all such applications were dismissed by the regular appellate court. Thereafter, the 4" respondent herein moved the Deputy Commissioner to invoke his power of review in terms of Section 25 of the Act. As a consequence of such an application being filed at the hands of the 4" respondent, the Deputy Aagrt Cafpmissioner has passed the impugned order on ‘é Vas i Mice. “i, Nore ZR UF B78 os

Ta fees NC: 2024: KHC:14503 s, WP No. 14741 of 2020 02.11.2020 at Annexure-A, recalling his own order while allowing the review petition and directing the respondent- Tahsildar to enter the name of the 4‘ respondent on the strength of the registered sale deed.

5. Learned Counsel for the petitioner submits that the impugned order cannot be sustained, because the Deputy Commissioner could not have passed the impugned order reviewing his own order invoking Section 25 of the Act. Moreover, it is submitted that the earlier v order of the Deputy Commissioner directing the restoration of the name of the original owner Smt. Padma Hegde in the land records was in accordance with law, having regard to the fact that a regular appeal was pending consideration and therefore, the parties would have to await the final outcome of the regular appeal.

6. The learned Counsel would also further bring to notice of this Court that the 4° respondent filed two suits

“Ep _3F Challenging the orders passed by the Deputy iS ~SeNissioner and such suits were dismissed on the r] Mes 4

; SoD “ Of sia WO) NC: 2024: KHC:14503 fins te tae, WP No. 14741 of 2020 ORR ob Set ground of the bar provided in the Act, wherein the powers for passing orders in respect of the revenue entries were exclusively within the domine of the revenue authorities and the civil courts were barred from entertaining any such plea regarding the revenue entries.
7. Per contra, learned Counsel for the 4" respondent - sought to place reliance on a decision of this Court in the = ¥ case of Sri. Lakshman Vs. State of Karnataka and others, reported in ILR 1995 KAR. 1871, wherein it was held that Sections 24 and 25 of the Act did not put any fetters affecting the inherent powers of the revenue courts to make such orders as may be necessary to meet the ends of justice or prevent abuse of the court.
8. The learned Counsel would further submit that having regard to the express provisions contained in Section 128 of the Act, since the 4" respondent acquired title over the land in question under a_ registered document, the 4" respondent is legally entitled to have his = Sr >;
ASST Meaip entered in the land records.
(s/ eee 2.1 ee ee

ok.
Meat ete NC: 2024: KHC:14503 fig Soe WP No. 14741 of 2020 F
9. Having heard the learned Counsels for the, petitioner as well as the contesting 4"" respondent and on perusing the petition papers this Court is of the considered opinion that the impugned order passed by the Deputy Commissioner invoking Section 25 of the Act to review his own order cannot be sustained. This Court has held in the case of Sri. Nataraj K and others Vs. State of Karnataka and others, in W. P. No.6357/2023 dated 06.03.2024, having regard to several other precedents that such power of review may be exercised, on the discovery of new and important matter or evidence which, after exercise of due diligence was not within the knowledge of the person seeking review or could not be produced by him at the time when the order was passed; it may be exercised where some mistake or error apparent on the face of the record is found; it may also be exercised on any analogous ground. Further, having regard to a more recent decisions of the Honb’‘le Supreme Court in the case of Naresh
a. and others Vs. Government (NCT of Dethi) fo fe en = iS a ted in (2019) 9 SCC 416 and Kapra Mazdoor Ekta wel hea? / ON Me / we ee a Dau”
WO HOY

q Of eto No NC: 2024: KHC:14503 ie Rec WP No. 14741 of 2020 Union Vs. Birla Cotton Spinning and Weaving Mills Ltd., and Another, reported in (2005) 13 SCC 777, it has been held that where court or quasi judicial authority having jurisdiction to adjudicate on merit proceeds to do so, its judgment or order can be reviewed on merit only if the court or the quasi judicial authority is vested with power of

review by express provision or by necessary implication.
¥v 10. Having regard to the said judgments of the Hon’ble Supreme Court, this Court has held that it is clear that the quasi judicial authorities are not permitted to review their orders unless such powers are expressly vested in them by the statute. The provision in Section 25 only enables the revenue authorities to invoke the inherent power to make such orders as may be necessary for the ends of justice or to prevent the abuse of process of the revenue court. It was therefore held that the provisions contained in Sections 24 or 25 do not expressly provide for review of an order passed by the revenue

- ACERT Riinaities, “es ha ty he eis S Ba Cine — i

-10 OF stmos0 NC: 2024: KHC:14503 bs Sears WP No. 14741 of 2020
11. In that view of the matter, the impugned order cannot be sustained. This Court is also of the considered opinion that the previous order passed by the Deputy Commissioner in the Revision Petition is to be upheld because it would maintain the revenue entries in the name of the original owner and therefore, till there is a final decision of the /is between the parties, in the suit and the appeal, the revenue authorities are required to be maintain the revenue records in the name of original Vv owner Smt. Padma Hegde.
12. Consequently, the writ petition is allowed. The impugned order dated 02.11.2020 at Annexure-A passed by the respondent-Deputy Commissioner, Udupi District, invoking Section 25 of the Act, is hereby quashed and set aside. In terms of the order passed by the Deputy Commissioner dated 11.12.2017, in C. DIS. R. A. P.
36/2016-17, the revenue entries shall be maintained in the name of Smt. Padma Hedge, till the outcome of the i FESUBY appeal in R. A. No.3/2015 which is pending 7 4, hy &, <4 py car a \wt Sf ging Maety VEE

le Nels NC: 2024: KHC:14503 pee WP No. 14741 of 2020 BS) eee!
consideration on the file of Additional District and Sessions Judge at Kundapura or pending consideration before the court where it is transferred in view of the jurisdiction.
Ordered accordingly.

AGREEMENT AND KARAR DEED: 15-6-2009

This Agreement regarding the sale of immoveable property after consent between both parties.

Kishan Hegde son of K. R. Hegde residing as Kolke Bailu Siriyara village and post Udupi Taluk, Udupi District having power of attorney from Padma Hegde daughter of Leela Hegde residing at Yadathadi village, Udupi Taluk as first party and

Jayaprakash D. Karkikara son of Dayananda Karkikara, residing

r opposite Syndicate Bank, Murudeshwara Main Road, Bhatkala Taluk, Karwar District as second party; witnesses:

The place described in the schedule below being the schedule property which has come to the name of the first party's, principal out of us as per the partition deed in their family on date: 5-1-2008, registered partition deed No.2552/2007-08 in the office of the Sub- Registrar, Brahmavar to the name of the 1" party’s principal out of us, said place is being enjoyed by the 1" party’s principals out of us by personal occupation having pahani records.

While things stand thus, due to necessity of funds for the 1”

4 party’s principal out of us, being interested to sell the below mentioned property, while was searching for customers, as the 2"

party out of us found this to be beneficials, being desirous of the said ‘ place, 2" party having come forward to purchse from the 1” party, fixing the sale for Rs.6,00,000-00(rupees six lakhs only) decided by the family persons as market value, 2”" party having agreed to obtain sale as per the same, this agreement deed has been entered into

after both parties discussing and eciding.

Specific conditions of this agreement deed are as follows:
Having decided that the total sale consideration will be Rs.6,00,000-00 for which the 2” party has agreed to obtain the plan which is the subject matter of sale for sale, out of the said sale la consideration 2" party having paid Rs.5,50,000-00 (rupees five lakhs fifty thousand only) to the first party as advance, the istparty out of us has agreed having received it and the conditions of this agreement deed will be in force till the balance sale consideration or Rs.50,000/- (rupees fifty thousand only) is paid by the a party out of us to the 1" party out of us within one year of this agreement or till the registration is got done. As per the same, the 2 party has agreed to be liable to pay the full amount at the time of registration of the place of this agreement and get the sale deed executed out of his own expenses and 1” party out of us having delivered possession of the said schedule property to the 2” party on today itself dated:
15-6-2009, accordingly the 2" party has obtained possession of the = said schedule property.

2. 1" party having executed this agreement deed making 2° party to believe that no any type of loan or alienation has been made on the security of the place of this agreement, if such security or alienations are found, 1" party out of us only in responsive 2" party out of us will not be liable. 4. Market value of the place of this agreement having been decided as it is now, neither party shall have any right to contend that it is more or less. If contended like that, it will not be valid.

GENERAL POWER OF ATTORNEY
General Power of Attorney executed by Padma Hegde daughter of Leelavathi Shedthi alias Leela. N. Hegde aged about 30 years, residing in Yadathadi village, Udupi Taluk in favour of Kishan Hegde son of Late
K. R. Hegde aged about 41 years residing at Kolke Bailu Shiriyara Village, Udupi Taluk.
The property shown in the details below is the property which has come to only my full rights along with other properties (out of one fourth share) through a partition deed registered in number 2552:07- 08 book number one in Brahmavara registrar's office on 5-1-2008,
1) Relating to the said property, you shall, as my agent enter > into agreement or agreements regarding giving for sale to person you find appropriate, with conditions you find appropriate and you shall obtain advance amount. You shall sign the said agreement or agreements as my agent, you are hereby authorised in this regard.

2) You shall sell the said property to the persons you find appropriate, for consideration or considerations you find appropriate, with conditions you find appropriate, you shall obtain consideration or considerations. You shall sign as my agent to the sale instrument or sale instruments which will be prepared in the name of the concerned person or concerned persons pertaining to the said property. You shall give up

a possession of the said property to the personal occupation of the concerned persons. You shall sign the necessary forms,

affidavits etc., you shall give consent as my agent regarding

3 the registration of the sale instrument or sale instruments in the presence of the Sub-Registrar in the office of the Sub- Registrar, Brahmavar, you shall also given consent as my agent regarding Khatha transfer regarding the said property in the name or names of the concerned. You shall perform any other necessary functions, you are authorised by this in this regard.

3) You shall obtain as my agent the sketch or sketches from the concerned office for the said property which are to be enclosed to the said sale instrument or sale instruments. You shall, as my agent sign the application etc., to be submitted in

. this regard and you shall pay the necessary charges, you are hereby authorised regarding all this.

4) You shall, as my agent convert the said property in entirety or in part for non-agricultural purpose. You shall as my agent sign the application which will be submitted in this regard and you shall pay the charges in this regard. You shall give the necessary affidavit etc., you shall obtain the said conversion order or orders. You are hereby authorised regarding all this.

5) am liable to agree that have myself performed the above said acts which you will perform as my agent on the basis of this regarding the said property and should ratify all of

7 them, ic re

For this reason the appeal is_ not maintainable in this Court. All the contentions raised by the revision petitioner z in his petition are untrue and untenable in law.

In S. No. 148/P1, at Yadthadi village 1.88 in (S. No.148/28 of 1.88 Acre) Acre on 15-06-2009 the said Smt Padma Hegde D/o Leela Hegde is the claimant of the land has given G. P. ’ to encumber the schedule property, the suit is under investigation and during the investigation.
Padma Hegde was ordered by the court to not attend another G. P. A. By letter through his brother Nikhila Hegde, he has filed an application before the court through his brother Nikhila Hegde, who is a witness and a witness.
The Hon’ble Court was rejected the application filed by the Nikhila Hegade in 13-06-2014 and 17-06-2014, Held, the present case stands for

final order.
In the meantime, despite the court's order id due to fraud, Padma Hegde's lawyer Shiriyar Muddanna. Shetty and lawyer P. S. Rao, who appeared through Nikhila Hegde, fraudulently concealed all matters in violation of the court's order and Advocate Shiriyar Muddanna Shetty was rejected by the court the Nikhila Hegde using GPA letter dated 08-06-2015 at Sub- Registrar Office of Brahmavar, and registered as Document no. 1108/2015-16. requested to record and provide justice.

IN THE COURT OF ASSISTANT COMMISSIONER
KUNDAPURA SUB DIVISION KUNDAPURA
NO: RRT(2) SR 31/2016-17 Date: 25-7-I6 Appellants Jayaprakash T. Karkikera S/o Dayananda Karkikera Near Murudeshwar Syndicate Bank (Represented by Advocate
T. G. Rao, Kundapur Uttara Kannada District) Respondents
1. Honorable Special Tahsildars a Brahmavar, Udupi Taluk
2. Shiriyar Muddanna Shetty S/o Duggappa Shetty Shiriyar Village Udupi Taluk and Distt The appellant has appealed against the order No. DMR 56/2015-16 dated 22-03-2016 passed by the Ist respondent under Section 136(2) of the Karnataka Land Revenue Act, 1964.
(Hereinafter referred to as the Order in question) This appeal involves 1.88 acres of land (as shawn in the appeal) in No. 148/28 (148P1) of > Page Lof7

Yadthadi Village, Udupi Taluk. (Hereinafter

referred to as the suit land)

Notice was duly served on the appellants and the respondents.

The appellants have sought to set aside the

impugned order for the following reasons.

1. Kishan Hegde, who has a power of attorney from the original owner of the land in question, Padma Hegde, has written a letter of agreement for sale to the appellant and since the above agreement has not been ho registered as per the condition, suit no.

27/2012 filed suit.

2. From the hearing of the above suit, the Hon'ble Court restrained the immovable Property from being encumbered by an interim order, and then on 15-07-2015 granted the suit and issued a final order to register the immovable property to the appellant.

3. In the meantime, in suit No. 27/2012 of the Hon'ble Court, the 2nd respondent has

pre Page 2 of 7

. pleaded on behalf of Padma Hegde, the owner of the land, and though there is an interim order while the suit is under investigation, the said land has been purchased by Nikala Hegde, who is again authorized by Padma Hegde, in his name.
This is not correct.

4. The appellant filed a criminal case in the Hon'ble Court against the 2nd respondent.
Under investigation at the police station as per court order.

3 5S. Appellant written not to change account as registered purchase. Despite filing the objection, the lst respondent has changed the account as per the impugned order. This is an illegal order.

6. The appeal is therefore allowed, the impugned order is set aside and remanded. In the name of Padma Hegde, who is the original owner of the land, the Pahani has been registered and the order has been sought.

Page 3 of 7

LJ?

Counsel for the 2nd respondent objected to the appeal as follows, seeking dismissal of the appeal.

1. The appellant has given a false statement in the appeal as stated in the appellant's

contract letter No. 148/P1 is 1.68 Acres, the Pahani Gazette of this place does not exist.
In this case the NOTAR applicant cannot get the deed and Padma Hegde will not issue the GPA to the Kishan Hegde.

2. The Appellant has filed a false suit in
O. S. No. 27/2012 in the Hon'ble Kundapur ™ Civil Court, the final decree order dated 15- 07-2015 will be true. Although the 2nd respondent had acquired the land in question. He is not made a defendant in this case.

3. The RA 3/2015 filed by Padma Hegade against the O. S No. 27/2012 Kundapura Civil Court, the restraining order passed by the Additional District Court.

Page 4 of 7 a”

11/ ~s 4. Registration obtained by the appellant in the Civil Court by the 2nd _ respondent Documentation and mutation order are not questioned.
Therefore, the appeal is sought to be dismissed.
Arguments were heard and records available were perused. Now the question before me is, Whether the questioned order of the respondent is orderly?
Answer:- Yes.

Answer: - Yes.

Reasons:- (1) An appeal under Karnataka Land

Revenue Act 1964 section 126(2) is allowed in

law against an order passed under section 129 of

the Act.

2. But the appellant has filed an appeal against the counterclaim filed by the respondent. This is not an order.

N Page 5 of 7

pre
3. Appellant has a letter of authority from wi Padma Hegde and the letter of agreement, written by Kishan Hegde has been verified.
It is found that a deed has been written for 1.88 acres in S. No. 148/P1 of Yadthadi Village.
4. The copy of the order of O. S No. 27/2012 of the Kundapur Senior Civil Court has been examined, vide letter of agreement dated 15- 06-2009 in No.148/51 it was found that the lst respondent was ordered to issue a purchase deed within 30 days of 1.88 acres le of land.
9. Padma Hegde against the above O. S No.
27/2012 Kundapur Additional District an appeal has been filed in the court vide RA 3/2015 and it appears that the injunction has been granted for 1 month from the hearing or till the final order.
6. On perusal of the copy of the registered purchase deed No. 1108/2015-16, it is found that the land in question was purchased by the 2nd respondent from Page 6 of 7 al

(a.
IPS
“ Nikala Hegde, who had the power of attorney of Padma Hegde.

7. On perusal of the copy of the said order DMR 56/2015-16, it is found that change of account has been ordered for the land in question as a registered purchase document.
It is orderly.

8. The appellant and the 2nd respondent are at liberty to seek appropriate relief as per the final order vide RA3/2015.

In this background the following order of the “ Civil Court
-: ORDER:-
Appeal dismissed.

This Order was announced in the open court on 25-1-11

proper action.

THIS DAY, THE COURT MADE THE FOLLOWING: "
. ORDER.;
The “petitioner-Smt. Padma Hegde and = Sn.
Jayaprakash D. Karkikar and thelr respective counsels are present before the court.
2. The parties have filed a joint memo stating that they have compromised the matter, therefore, the court can record the compromise and quash the proceedings, as prayed for,
3. It Is stated in the joint memo that, pursuant to s withdrawal of the allegations made by the respondent against the petitioner in C. cC. No,1235/2016, he has no objection for quashing of the entire proceedings pending on + the file of If Additional Civil Judge and JMFC, Kundapura.
It is also stated that the respondent has withdrawn ail the . allegations made against the petitioner herein in the said criminal case, which is pending before the said court, 4, The allegations made in the charge sheet Papers are with reference to a civil litigation between the:
parties, It is alleged that the petitioner has agreed to / Sete, Ae é wrancke ae 3 v

f? 4
2. 3 y, execute the sale deed on the basis of the sgreement, but refused to do the sarmne and subsequently, they executed a ——— ] mt sale deed in favour of third person when a civil case was L << niin if pending and a prohibitory order was operating. Therefore, . on such allegations, the offences were alleged under - Section 177 and 420 rfw. 34 of IPC. Now the matter is settled between the parties. As I have already stated, it is a dispute between the parties and now it is settled. ome 2 Therefore, there is no legal impediment to quash the proceedings. In this context, it is worth to mention here a ¥ decision of Hon'ble Apex Court rendered in Gian Singh Vs.
State of Punjab and Another [ (2012) 10 SCC 303], wherein the Hon'ble Apex Court has given certain guidelines with regard to quashing of the proceedings whenever the ' parties have entered inta compromise. The relevant portion of the said decision reads thus:- "Held -Power of High Court In quashing a:
criminal -preceeding or FIR or cormplaint in exercise of its inherent jurisdiction is distinct and different from power of a criminal court of:
compounding offences under S, 320 - Cases . where power to quash criminal proceedings may en be exercised where the parties have settled their dispute, held, depends on facts and circurmstances FB thin
5 E I

S b 4 ' of each case - Before exercise of inhererit i gquashment power under 5.482, High Court must have due regard to nature and gravity of the:
crime and its socetal impact. 22.00...
Thus, held, heinous and serious offences of mental depravity, murder, rape, dacoity, etc., oF under special statutes like Prevention of, Corruption Act or offences committed by public servants, cannot be quashed even though victim or victim's family and offender have settled the dispute — Such offences are not private in nature and have a serious impact on society.
XXX XMM OXNX XMM XK XX
“But criminal cases having overwhelmingly e and predaminatingly civil Mavour stand on a different footing Offences. arising from commercial financial, mercantile, civil, partnership or like transactions or offences arising out of matrimony relating to dowry, etc. or family disputes where the wrong is basically private or personal in nature and parties have resolved their envire dispute, High Court may quash criminal proceedings - High Court, in such cases, must consider whether it would-be unfair or contrary to interest of justice to cantinue with the criminal.
proceeding or continuation of criminal proceeding would tantamount to abuse of process of law ‘ despite settlement and compromise between ee ee parties and whether to secure ends cf justice, it is ‘3 t See TS] = SS ——

/ DFR
appropriate the criminal case it put to an end. If such question(s) are answered in the affirmative, High Court shal! be well within its jurisdiction to quash the criminal proceedinas..,”
5. This case alse falls within the categories of the cases as per the guidelines of the Hon'ble Apex Court in the decision cited supra. Therefore, there is no legal impediment to quash the proceedinas as prayed for.
Hence, the following order:-
ORDER
The petition is allowed. Joint Memo filed by i 4 the parties is accepted. Consequently, the case in
C. C, No.1235/2016 registered against the petitioner for the offences punishable under Sections 420 and 177 r/w. 34 of IPC and pending on the file of the Il Addl. Civil Judge and IMFC, Kundapura and all further proceedings therein, in so far as this petitioner concerned, are hereby quashed.

Appellants hereby differs that:-

In the Respondents the 1% Respondent in Survey No. 148/7 extent of.39 1/2 Acre of Yadthadi village, granted Power of Attorney on 16-05-2009 the GPA is given, on 16-05-2009 Padma Hegde Survey No.148/7 extent 8 % cents sold to Ramu S. Shetty for the said sale deed the

Page 2 of 13:

2 £fp ; Kishan Hegde signed as a witness. Thereafter on 15-06-2009 in respect of the non-existent Survey No. 148/P1 extent of 1.88 Acres he created a duplicate deed form on Rs.100 stamp paper enclosed herewith. The lst to 6th, 7th and 8th respondents have been making enmity against the appellant with the lure of money by keeping the fake contract as a document.
MAIN POINT OF THE CASE:

Duggappa Shetty, aged about 66 years, R/at. Shiriyara Village & Post, Udupi Taluk and District, PIN-567 210, today at Bengaluru, do hereby solemnly affirm and state on oath as follows:- 4: I submit that, I was respondent No.4 in the Writ Petition No. 14741/2020 (KLR-RES), and also party in the earlier proceedings before the Revenue Authorities, and in view of the same I am fully acquainted with the facts and circumstances of the case. Hence, I am competent to make _ this 2 application and affidavit for stay of the impugned order dated: 08.04.2024 passed in W. P. No.

' 14741/202 (KLR-RES), passed by the Ld. Single Judge, in the said writ petition.
2. I submit and state that the facts, circumstances and the grounds set-out in the Memorandum of Writ Appeal, whereby the judgment dated: 08.04.2024 passed by the Learned Single Judge in the
W. P. No.14741/2020 (KLR-RES) has been impugned, may kindly be considered as part and parcel of this application and affidavit as if the same are reproduced herein specifically.
3. I humbly state and submit that the writ petition . filed by the respondent No.1 is not maintainable, in as much as, the basis for filing the writ petition for quashing the order dated: 02-11-2020, passed by respondent No.2 in Case No. C. Dis. RAP./ SR/33/2019, (Annexure-A) to the writ petition, is that he obtained an Agreement of Sale dated: 15- 06-2009 from the agent of Smt. Padma Hegde and as she did not execute the sale deed, suit being
O. S. No.27/2012, was filed for specific performance and such suit was decreed by the Civil Court at Kundapura vide judgment and decree dated:
15.07.2015. It is stated that- be a) the property described in the power of attorney given by Smt. Padma Hegde to her

: agent was in respect of land bearing Sy. No.148/7 measuring 2 acres 78 cents, situated in Yadathadi grama, Brahmavara Taluk, Udupi District, but in the plaint in
0. S. No.27/2012 and the decree passed by the Civil Court refer to suit property as Sy. No.148/P1 measuring 1 acre 88 cents of Yadathadi grama, Brahmavara Taluk, Udupi District. In the premises, the identity of the

property was not established,

b) Agreement of sale dated: 15-06-2009 was an inadmissible document, in as much as,

‘ the document was not stamped as required under Section 5-G of the Karnataka Stamp Act. Particularly, as the possession of the property was delivered under the agreement

to the respondent No.1,

c) said agreement of sale dated: 15.06.2009 was not registered under the provisions of the Registration Act, more so, when the possession of the property was given to the respondent No.1.

Hon'ble Trial Court which passed the decree in O. S. No.27/2012, did not take into

ra consideration the above aspects and therefore such a decree does not confer any

: right in favour respondent No.1 and therefore, respondent No.1 has no right to question, the sale deed in favour of the

appellant in respect of property bearing Sy. No.148/28 measuring 1 acre 88 cents of Yadathadi grama, Brahmavara Taluk, Udupi District, and also question the orders of the revenue authorities making Khatha of the said property in the name of the appellant.
4. 1 further state and submit that said Smt. Padma Hegde questioned the judgment and decree dated:
3 15.07.2015 passed in O. S. No.27/2012 by the court of Civil Judge, Kundapura by filing R. A No.3/2015, in the court of Addl. District and Sessions Judge at Udupi, where there is a stay of the Trial Court decree. It is further submitted that the respondent No.1 had filed criminal case in C. C. No.1235/2016, in the court of Judicial Magistrate 1° Class Kundapura against Smt. Padma Hegde, for the offences punishable under Sections 177, 420, R/w 34 of IPC. However, the respondent No.1 and the said Smt. Padma Hegde, to defeat the rights of the appellant in respect of the said property, compromised the matter in Cri. Petn. No, 8926/2017 4 by obtaining order from the Hon’ble High Court.
These facts clearly establish collusion between

4 respondent No.1 and Smt. Padma Hegde to defeat the right of the appellant.

5; I further state and submit that, the review petition of the appellant was accepted by the respondent No.2 vide order dated: 02-11-2020 passed in
C. Dis. RAP/ SR/33/2019, (Annexure-A) to the writ petition, as the Deputy Commissioner found that in the earlier order dated: 11.12.2017, there was observation to the effect that the sale deed in favour of appellant was invalid. Further, respondent No.2, as Revenue Court had power to correct its order in the exercise of powers under the Karnataka

Yi Land Revenue Act.

6. I submit that I have a fairly arguable and good case in the appeal, and therefore, this is a fit and proper case for staying the impugned order/judgment dated: 08.04.2024 of the Ld. Single Judge passed in
W. P. No.14741/2020 (KLR-RES), otherwise I shall suffer irreparable hardship and injury. Infact, on the

basis of the impugned order respondent No.1 and the said Smt. Padma Hegde are likely to change RTC entry in respect of suit property and in that event I will suffer untold misery and hardship.

7. I further state and submit that, this is a fit and

proper case for allowing the stay application to advance justice to me.

I
WHEREFORE, I most humbly pray that this Hon’ble Court be pleased to stay the operation § and implementation of the impugned judgment/order dated:
08.04.2024 passed by the Learned Single Judge in
W. P. No.14741/2020 (KLR-RES), pending disposal of the appeal on merits at the ex-parte stage, in the interest of justice and equity.
That for this act of kindness, I shall ever pray for.
